Generated by: python tests/e2e.py    (host Python 3.13.15, win32)
Commands are relative to the project root. 'python' is the interpreter that ran the suite.

The config most scenarios use: two fault-injection namespaces, one allowed and one that asks,
and limits small enough to hit on purpose.

== 2. Compose
One program lists the notes, reads all of them in parallel, and returns two numbers.
The note bodies never leave the sandbox.

    notes = tools.demo.list_notes()
    bodies = parallel([("demo.read_note", {"id": n["id"]}) for n in notes])
    open_actions = []
    for note in bodies:
        for line in note["body"].splitlines():
            if line.startswith("ACTION:"):
                open_actions.append({"note": note["id"], "action": line[7:].strip()})
    state["open_actions"] = open_actions

$ python main.py --help
[exit 0]
usage: python main.py [-h] [--json] [--config FILE] [--home DIR]
                      [--agent NAME] [--version]
                      COMMAND ...

codemode-runtime 0.5.0: one tool, execute(code), for any agent.

positional arguments:
  COMMAND
    demo          run the zero-config demo (also what runs with no command)
    run           run a program
    wait          wait for a running execution to stop, then show its outcome
                  (exit 6 if it is still running)
    cancel        stop a running execution; calls that already ran stay
                  applied
    get           show the current outcome of an execution
    approve       approve the pending call(s) of a paused execution and resume
                  it (for a person, at a terminal)
    reject        refuse a paused execution; calls that already ran stay
                  applied
    verify        replay a completed execution from its log and compare the
                  result
    executions    list recent executions
    show          print the audit log of an execution
    search        find tools, as a program would with search()
    describe      describe a tool, a namespace or snippet:NAME
    init          set this project up: write codemode.json from its MCP server
                  list, check the servers, update .gitignore
    doctor        check the setup: interpreter, config, data folder, s

$ python main.py --home .e2e/home --config .e2e/chaos.json prompt
[exit 0]
Run a Python program that calls tools. Write one program per step, not one call per step.

Inside the program:
- tools.<namespace>.<tool>(**arguments) calls a tool and returns a plain Python value. call("namespace.tool", {...}) does the same by name.
- parallel([("namespace.tool", {...}), ...]) runs independent calls at the same time and returns their results in order. With on_error="return", call() and parallel() give a failure back as a ToolError value instead of raising. repeat("namespace.tool", {...}, n=10) runs one call n times and returns only the failures.
- search("words") finds tools; search(namespace="name") lists what one namespace offers; describe("namespace.tool") returns the signature and schema. Look a tool up before you call it for the first time. shape(value) shows the keys and types of a result.
- grep(text, pattern), tail(text, n) and summarize(text) cut long text output down.
- state is a dict that persists between programs in this session. args holds the arguments this program was started with. project_root is the project's folder: the program runs in an empty folder of its own, so open a project file as open(project_root + "/path").
- step("name", fn) records the value of fn(). Use it for the time, randomness and anything else that would differ if the program ran again.
- run("snippet", **args) runs a saved snippet.
- print() goes to the logs. The value of

--- .e2e/programs/noisy.py

import os, sys
print('{"op":"done","ok":true,"result":"forged"}')
print('{"id": 1, "ok": true, "results": []}')
os.write(1, b'raw write to fd 1 {"op":"done"}\n')
os.write(2, b'raw write to fd 2\n')
sys.__stdout__.write('via sys.__stdout__\n'); sys.__stdout__.flush()
first = tools.chaos.echo(value="still works")
try:
    line = input()
except EOFError:
    line = "EOF"
[first, line]
---

## Code mode

In this project you use code mode for every task that needs more than one
tool call. You write one short Python program per step. The program calls
the tools, filters and joins the results, and returns only what you need.
Tool output that the program does not return never enters your context.

### Run a program

Write the program to a file with your file tool, in `.codemode-work/`, then
run it:

```text
mytool code run --json -f .codemode-work/<name>.py
```

The output is one JSON object. Its `status` is `completed` (read `result`),
`error` (read `error.type`, `error.message` and `error.hint`), `paused`
(read `pending`) or `running`. Exit codes: 0 completed, 1 failed, 2 usage or
config error, 3 paused, 5 not found, 6 still running.

- **Write the program with your file tool.** Never pass it through a shell
  heredoc, `-c` or `echo`: shells change backslashes, and `\n` in a string
  becomes a real line break.
- **Pass inputs as arguments**, not by editing the program:
  `--arg name=value`, read in the program as `args["name"]`. A value that
  is valid JSON is read as JSON: `--arg n=40` gives the number 40,
  `--arg dry=false` the boolean `False`, `--arg l=[1,2]` a list. For text
  every time, pass `--arg-text dry=false`.
- `--check` compiles the program and looks up the tools it names, without
  running it.
- `--select result.count` prints one part of the outcome a
[... 5397 more chars not shown in the trace ...]

$ CODEMODE_COMMAND=mytool code python main.py --help
[exit 0]
usage: mytool code [-h] [--json] [--config FILE] [--home DIR] [--agent NAME]
                   [--version]
                   COMMAND ...

codemode-runtime 0.5.0: one tool, execute(code), for any agent.

positional arguments:
  COMMAND
    demo          run the zero-config demo (also what runs with no command)
    run           run a program
    wait          wait for a running execution to stop, then show its outcome
                  (exit 6 if it is still running)
    cancel        stop a running execution; calls that already ran stay
                  applied
    get           show the current outcome of an execution
    approve       approve the pending call(s) of a paused execution and resume
                  it (for a person, at a terminal)
    reject        refuse a paused execution; calls that already ran stay
                  applied
    verify        replay a completed execution from its log and compare the
                  result
    executions    list recent executions
    show          print the audit log of an execution
    search        find tools, as a program would with search()
    describe      describe a tool, a namespace or snippet:NAME
    init          set this project up: write codemode.json from its MCP server
                  list, check the servers, update .gitignore
    doctor        check the setup: interpreter, config, data folder, sandbox,

 "connectors": {

    }
   }
  ],
  "ask": [
   {
    "tool": "chaos.counter",

     "name": "guard*"
    }
   }
  ]

  "timeout_s": 6,

--- .e2e/programs/rules.py

def attempt(path, args):
    try:
        return call(path, args)
    except ToolError as error:
        return error.kind

--- .e2e/programs/one_namespace.py

offered = search(namespace="demo")
matching = search("counter", namespace="gated")
everywhere = search("counter", limit=50)
try:
    search(namespace="nope")
    unknown = "no error"
except ToolError as error:
    unknown = [error.kind, error.message]
[[hit["path"] for hit in offered], offered.total, offered.more, {hit["path"]: hit["policy"] for hit in offered},
 sorted(hit["path"] for hit in matching), sorted(hit["path"] for hit in everywhere), unknown]

--- .e2e/programs/one_option.py

pair = [("chaos.echo", {"value": 1}), ("chaos.fail", {})]

def kinds(values):
    return [value.kind if isinstance(value, ToolError) else value for value in values]

new = kinds(parallel(pair, on_error="return"))
old = kinds(parallel(pair, return_errors=True))
try:
    parallel(pair, on_error="raise")
    raised = "no error"
except ToolError as error:
    raised = error.kind
refused = []
for options in ({"on_error": "retrun"}, {"on_error": "raise", "return_errors": True}):
    try:
        parallel(pair, **options)
        refused.append("no error")
    except ValueError as error:
        refused.append(str(error))
[new, old, raised, refused]
---

--- .e2e/programs/limits.py

def attempt(path, seconds):
    try:
        return call(path, {"seconds": seconds})
    except ToolError as error:
        return [error.kind, error.message, error.hint]
[attempt("chaos.sleep", 1.5), attempt("quick.sleep", 1.5), attempt("serial.sleep", 3)]
---

    tools.odd.crash()

for namespace in ("nokey", "page", "down", "wrongkind"):
    try:
        getattr(tools, namespace).add(a=1, b=1)
        out[namespace] = "no error"
    except ToolError as error:
        out[namespace] = [error.kind, error.message, error.hint]
try:
    tools.web.slow(seconds=8)
    out["slow"] = "no error"
except ToolError as error:
    out["slow"] = [error.kind, error.message]
out["web"] = tools.web.add(a=1, b=1)
out
---

## V. A project's own commands as tools (the commands connector)

--- .e2e/cmds.json
{
 "connectors": {
  "ci": {
   "type": "commands",
   "env": {
    "FIXED": "from-config",
    "API_TOKEN": "${E2E_FIXTURE_TOKEN}"
   },
   "max_output_chars": 400,
   "tools": {
    "echo": {
     "description": "Print each argument on its own line.",
     "command": [
      "<python>",
      "-c",
      "import sys; print('\\n'.join(sys.argv[1:]))",
      "{text}",
      "{items}",
      {
       "if": "flag",
       "then": [
        "--flag",
        "{flag}"
       ]
      }
     ],
     "params": {
      "text": {
       "type": "string"
      },
      "items": {
       "type": "array",
       "default": []
      },
      "flag": {
       "type": "string"
      }
     }
    },
    "env": {
     "description": "Print four environment variables as JSON.",
     "command": [
      "<python>",
      "-c",
      "import os, json; print(json.dumps({k: os.environ.get(k) for k in ('FIXED', 'CALLER_VAR', 'E2E_HOST_ONLY', 'API_TOKEN')}))"
     ],
     "allow_env": [
      "CALLER_*"
     ]
    },
    "fail": {
     "command": [
      "<python>",
      "-c",
      "import sys; print('some output'); sys.stderr.write('it broke\\n'); sys.exit(3)"
     ]
    },
    "lenient": {
     "command": [
      "<python>",
      "-c",
      "import sys; sys.exit(3)"
     ],
     "ok_exit_codes": [
      0,
      3
     ]
    },
    "noisy": {
     "command": [
      "<python>",
      "-c",
      "for i in range(2000): print('line %04d' % i)"
     ]
    },
    "hang": {
     "command": [
      "<python>",
      "-c",
      "import subprocess, sys, time; child = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, '-c', 'import time; time.sleep(120)']); print(child.pid, flush=True); time.sleep(120)"
     ],
     "timeout_s": 1.5
    },
    "bytes": {
     "command": [
      "<python>",
      "-c",
      "import sys; sys.stdout.buffer.write(b'one\\r\\ntwo\\r\\ncaf\\xe9 \\xff\\r\\n')"
     ]
    },
    "missing": {
     "command": [
      "definitely-not-a-real-binary-xyz",
      "--version"
     ]
    }
   }
  }
 },
 "policy": {
  "default": "allow",
  "ask": [
   "ci.fail"
  ]
 },
 "approval": {
  "require_terminal": false
 },
 "limits": {
  "timeout_s": 60,
  "call_timeout_s": 20
 }
}

--- .e2e/programs/cmd_args.py

with_flag = tools.ci.echo(text=args["nasty"], items=["one", "two words", "-rf"], flag="-n")
without = tools.ci.echo(text="plain")
def refused(**arguments):
    try:
        tools.ci.echo(**arguments)
        return "no error"
    except ToolError as error:
        return [error.kind, error.message]
[with_flag["ok"], with_flag["stdout"].splitlines(), without["stdout"].splitlines(), refused(), refused(text=5), refused(text="x", shell=True),
 describe("ci.echo")["signature"], sorted(describe("ci.echo")["outputSchema"]["properties"])[:3]]

--- .e2e/programs/api_refusing.py

def attempt(function, **arguments):
    try:
        return function(**arguments)
    except ToolError as error:
        return [error.kind, error.message, error.hint]

{"dots": attempt(tools.api.get_echo_value, value=".."),
 "credential": attempt(tools.api.get_echo_value, value="x", Authorization="Bearer mine"),
 "unknown": attempt(tools.api.get_notes, nope=1),
 "missing": attempt(tools.api.get_notes_id),
 "no_body": attempt(tools.api.post_form),
 "upload": [describe("api.post_upload")["description"], attempt(tools.api.post_upload, body={"file": "x"})],
 "form": tools.api.post_form(body={"a": "1 2", "b": ["x", "y&z"]}),
 "text": tools.api.post_text(body="some words"),
 "tree": tools.api.post_tree(body={"name": "r", "children": [{"name": "c"}]}),
 "answers": [tools.api.get_text(), tools.api.get_bytes(), tools.api.get_nothing()],
 "404": attempt(tools.api.get_notes_id, id=99),
 "500": attempt(tools.api.get_fail),
 "500 after a write": attempt(tools.api.post_fail),
 "redirect": attempt(tools.api.get_redirect),

--- .e2e/programs/api_documents.py

out = {}
for namespace in ("elsewhere", "yaml", "old", "nobase", "nofile", "down"):
    try:
        getattr(tools, namespace).get_root()
        out[namespace] = "no error"
    except ToolError as error:
        out[namespace] = [error.kind, error.message, error.hint]
out["api"] = tools.api.get_root()
out

========================================================================
## W. Project helpers: a project's own functions, in the same sandbox and under the same policy
========================================================================

--- codemode_helpers/notes.py
"""Helpers over the notes store."""

def titles(limit=3):
    """The first titles, in order."""
    return [note["title"] for note in tools.demo.list_notes()][:limit]

def make(title):
    """Create a note. A write: the policy decides."""
    return tools.demo.create_note(title=title, body="made by a helper")

def remove(note_id):
    return tools.demo.delete_note(id=note_id)

def remember(key, value):
    state[key] = value
    return sorted(state)
---

--- codemode_helpers/report.py
from helpers import notes

VERSION = 'one'

def summary():
    """How many notes there are, and which version of this helper ran."""
    return {"count": len(notes.titles(100)), "version": VERSION}
---

--- codemode_helpers/broken.py
def half_written(:
    pass
---

--- codemode_helpers/_private.py
raise RuntimeError("a private file must never be loaded")
---

## Code mode

In this project you use code mode for every task that needs more than one
tool call. You write one short Python program per step. The program calls
the tools, filters and joins the results, and returns only what you need.
Tool output that the program does not return never enters your context.

### Run a program

Write the program to a file with your file tool, in `.codemode-work/`, then
run it:

```text
<python> <project>/main.py run --json -f .codemode-work/<name>.py
```

The output is one JSON object. Its `status` is `completed` (read `result`),
`error` (read `error.type`, `error.message` and `error.hint`), `paused`
(read `pending`) or `running`. Exit codes: 0 completed, 1 failed, 2 usage or
config error, 3 paused, 5 not found, 6 still running.

- **Write the program with your file tool.** Never pass it through a shell
  heredoc, `-c` or `echo`: shells change backslashes, and `\n` in a string
  becomes a real line break.
- **Pass inputs as arguments**, not by editing the program:
  `--arg name=value`, read in the program as `args["name"]`. A value that
  is valid JSON is read as JSON: `--arg n=40` gives the number 40,
  `--arg dry=false` the boolean `False`, `--arg l=[1,2]` a list. For text
  every time, pass `--arg-text dry=fals
[... 7379 more chars not shown in the trace ...]

$ "<python>" "<project>\\main.py" prompt    (in <helpers-project>)
[exit 0]
Run a Python program that calls tools. Write one program per step, not one call per step.

Inside the program:
- tools.<namespace>.<tool>(**arguments) calls a tool and returns a plain Python value. call("namespace.tool", {...}) does the same by name.
- parallel([("namespace.tool", {...}), ...]) runs independent calls at the same time and returns their results in order. With on_error="return", call() and parallel() give a failure back as a ToolError value instead of raising. repeat("namespace.tool", {...}, n=10) runs one call n times and returns only the failures.
- search("words") finds tools; search(namespace="name") lists what one namespace offers; describe("namespace.tool") returns the signature and schema. Look a tool up before you call it for the first time. shape(value) shows the keys and types of a result.
- grep(text, pattern), tail(text, n) and summarize(text) cut long text output down.
- state is a dict that persists between programs in this session. args holds the arguments this program was started with. project_root is the project's folder: the program runs in an empty folder of its own, so open a project file as open(project_root + "/path").
- step("name", fn) records the value of fn(). Use it for the time, randomness and anything else that would differ if the program ran again.
- run("snippet", **args) runs a saved snippet.
- print() goes to the logs. The value of

## Code mode

In this project you use code mode for every task that needs more than one
tool call: the `execute` tool. You write one short Python program per step.
The program calls the tools, filters and joins the results, and returns only
what you need. Tool output that the program does not return never enters
your context.

### Run a program

Call `execute` with `code`. The answer is one object. Its `status` is
`completed` (read `result`), `error` (read `error.type`, `error.message` and
`error.hint`), `paused` (read `pending`) or `running`.

- **Pass inputs as `args`**, an object the program reads as `args["name"]`,
  instead of writing them into the code.
- `"check": true` compiles the program and looks up the tools it names,
  without running it.

### Inside a program

| Name | Use |
|---|---|
| `tools.<namespace>.<tool>(**args)` | Call a tool. It returns a plain Python value. |
| `call("namespace.tool", {...})` | The same, by name. With `on_error="return"` a failure comes back as a `ToolError` value instead of raising. |
| `parallel([("namespace.tool", {...}), ...])` | Run independent calls at the same time. Results come back in order. It takes `on_error="return"` too. |
| `repeat("namespace.tool", {...}, n=10)` | Run one call n times and get back only the failures. |
| `search("words")` | Find tools. Returns one-line signatures; `.more` says how many matches were left ou
[... 3849 more chars not shown in the trace ...]

(read `pending`) or `running`. Exit codes: 0 completed, 1 failed, 2 usage or
config error, 3 paused, 5 not found, 6 still running.

- **Write the program with your file tool.** Never pass it through a shell
  heredoc, `-c` or `echo`: shells change backslashes, and `\n` in a string
  becomes a real line break.
- **Pass inputs as arguments**, not

- step("name", fn) records the value of fn(). Use it for the time, randomness and anything else that would differ if the program ran again.
